Unlimited Phone Data – Check Your Heaviest Month Before You Pay for It

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

Before you pay for unlimited phone data, check how much cellular data you actually use. Your carrier’s app or past bills show it month by month, and if your heaviest recent month, hotspot included, fits under a cheaper plan with a data cap, switch to that plan and keep the difference.

Data your phone uses on Wi-Fi at home and at work never counts against your plan. If you’re on Wi-Fi most of the day, your phone may use only a few gigabytes of cellular data a month.

Start with your carrier’s count, since a data cap is measured against it. Its app or online account shows each line’s use by billing cycle, as do past bills, so look back three to six months to catch any heavy month.

Your phone’s settings show which apps used the data. On an iPhone, the Current Period total in Settings > Cellular counts all data since the last time anyone tapped Reset Statistics on that screen, so tap it on the day your billing cycle starts. On an Android phone, you can set the data usage cycle to start on your billing date.

Size the plan to your heaviest month, not your average, and make sure that month includes any hotspot use, which some carriers list separately. Then check whether a capped plan counts hotspot data against its monthly allowance or leaves hotspot out, as some cheap plans do.

Take a commuter whose carrier’s app shows about 6 GB in a typical month and 9 GB in her heaviest. She pays $65 a month for an unlimited line whose extras she wouldn’t miss. Switching to a prepaid plan with 15 GB on the same network, at $25 a month, covers her heaviest month and saves her $480 a year.

Going past a prepaid plan’s allowance usually slows your data or pauses it until you buy more, rather than adding a charge. The plan’s broadband label, a fact sheet the FCC requires for every plan, has to say which. Unlimited is worth paying for if you stream video away from Wi-Fi most days, often use your phone as a hotspot, take video calls on the road, or have a family whose data use swings from month to month.

Choose a plan whose allowance clears your heaviest month by a few gigabytes.

To find a plan with that allowance, compare the best cheap cell phone plans.

‘You Can See Everything’ Becomes Rare Documentary to Surpass $100 Million Globally

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

“You Can See Everything” has surpassed $100 million globally, a rare box office milestone for a documentary.

So far, the zeitgeist-capturing look at Theranos fraudster Elizabeth Holmes has generated a staggering $93.4 million at the domestic box office and $9 million overseas for a worldwide tally of $102.4 million. It continues to mint money for A24, which produced the film in secret for roughly $7 million. Related Stories The Business of Being a Business Manager; 'The Secret Reading Club of Kabul' Directors on Finding Inspiration and Courage From 'World of Warcraft Forever' Surprise to Le Sserafim, Simu Liu and T-Pain Appearances, How BlizzCon 2026 Set the Stage for Blizzard Entertainment's Next Era

Over the weekend, “You Can See Everything” opened in the United Kingdom with $3.2 million from 596 locations. Those ticket sales register as A24’s second-largest debut in the territory behind “Backrooms” ($5.7 million). Similar to in the United States, around 70% of the audience in the U.K. was under 35 years old. Australia and New Zealand is currently the top-grossing market with $3.8 million.

Elsewhere, the film is getting a staggered rollout at the international box office: it’s also playing in Turkey, Poland, Czech Republic, Russia, United Arab Emirates and Greece (contributing around $2 million in total), and it’ll debut over the next few weeks in other territories including Mexico, Brazil and Argentina (Nov. 26 and 25) Germany, Austria and Hong Kong (Dec. 3 and 4), Spain (Dec. 25), France (Dec. 30) and Japan (March 12). A24 is handling the release in select territories, with Sony distributing in markets including Denmark, Norway, Sweden and the Middle East.

Already, “You Can See Everything” is one of the biggest theatrical documentaries in years. It’ll rely on overseas audiences to topple the genre’s all-time record. Michael Moore’s seminal 2004 film “Fahrenheit 9/11” is the highest-grossing documentary in history with $222 million globally, including $119 million domestically and $103 million overseas; followed by “March of the Penguins” with $127 million globally, including $77 million domestically and $50 million overseas.

Directed by comedian Nathan Fielder and filmmaker Lance Oppenheim, “You Can See Everything” became a word-of-mouth smash after a surprise world premiere at Telluride Film Festival. The movie opened to $15 million domestically in mid October and, with sensational reviews, remained a big-screen draw throughout the fall. The nearly three-hour-long doc takes place over three years and begins as Holmes, the founder of the health technology company Theranos, is preparing for her 11-year prison sentence for defrauding investors.

Gabbriette Bechtel and Matty Healy have a new gig: parents! The 1975 frontman and model, musician, and Charli XCX muse are expecting their first child together, as per People’s report.

The couple were photographed on a grocery run at Bristol Farms in West Hollywood, with Bechtel showing a visible baby bump. Healy carried several shopping bags, dressed in a navy jacket, gray T-shirt, distressed jeans, and boots, while Bechtel wore a black midi dress with a plunging neckline and simple black ballet flats.

Bechtel and Healy were first romantically linked in September 2023, when they were photographed kissing in New York City. In June 2024, Bechtel confirmed their engagement on Instagram with a photograph of her custom black diamond ring, featuring an elongated cushion-cut stone, halo setting, and triple-split shank. Her announcement, made following Charli XCX’s Brat show in Brooklyn, was fittingly irreverent: “MARRYING THE 1975 IS VERY BRAT.”

The happy news comes just four months after the couple wed at Castillo del Lago, Madonna’s former Hollywood Hills estate, in July. For the occasion, Bechtel enlisted Matières Fécales to create three bridal looks: as shown in her bridal fitting with Vogue, these included a sculptural white silk taffeta wedding gown with a dramatic triple bustle and Stephen Jones veil, a reworked vintage Martin Margiela dress, and a leather after-party ensemble. “Bridal is whatever you want it to be,” she told Vogue at her final fitting in Paris.

Now, the couple are embarking on their next chapter together—and their biggest collaboration yet. Congratulations once more to Gabbriette and Matty.

Cardinale: ‘I still expect Milan to compete for the Scudetto’

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Gerry Cardinale says he still expects Milan to compete for the Scudetto. He also warned that the Rossoneri will only make a January signing if it’s one that genuinely makes a difference.

With quotes via MilanNews, the Milan owner spoke to Michael Cuomo of TeleLombardia after Sunday evening’s 5-1 win over Frosinone at San Siro. Adrien Rabiot set up two goals as five different players got on the scoresheet. The result lifted Ruben Amorim’s side to fourth on 27 points, three behind Roma and five off leaders Lazio.

“Well, I’m never satisfied, but the results speak for themselves. The boys were fantastic tonight,” Cardinale said.

“I hope you’re happy. I hope the fans are happy. I think the team is really coming together. It’s one game, so we keep the enthusiasm in check, but I’m really proud of the boys. This is a team, and it’s a team that wants to win right to the end.”

“Look, it’s November. We still have a long way to go, but we want to see improvements in every game. This weekend has been very interesting if you look at the results. You can see something is changing in Serie A.

“I was very happy to see us not only win, but win by that margin. We have to build on it. As Amorim says, every game is something to build on.

“Personally, I still expect us to compete for the Scudetto, but it’s a long way off. One game at a time. Now the focus is on the Europa League, and as Ruben said, we have to do both. European competitions are important, and everything is a test. If you want to win a Scudetto, you have to go all the way, and that’s what we’ll do.”

Milan now travel to Olympiacos on Thursday, with kick-off at 18:45 CET [17:45 GMT]. Amorim’s side have lost their last two league-phase games, against Bournemouth and Ferencváros, so there is little margin for error in Athens.

Cardinale was also asked whether a new player could arrive in January.

“Again, January is just another stage. As you know, the January market is complicated, so you have to be disciplined. You can’t just throw yourself at something at random.

“The one thing that will happen under my ownership is that whenever we enter the transfer market, we will play to win. That’s the theme I set out at the start of this year. So if we make a move in any window, January or June, it will be a move that counts.”

Quentin Tarantino and Wife Daniella Pick Welcome Baby No. 3

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Quentin Tarantino and his wife Daniella Pick shared that they have welcomed their third baby together, a boy, with the new addition joining their kids Leo, 6, and Ariana, 4.

Once upon a time… Quentin Tarantino became a father of three.

Four years after the birth of their second child, the Oscar winner and his wife Daniella Pick Tarantino welcomed their third baby, Quentin’s rep confirmed to People Nov. 22.

“Quentin Tarantino and his wife, Daniella Pick Tarantino, are overjoyed to announce the birth of their third child,” the rep said in a statement to the outlet, “a baby boy.”

The couple—who tied the knot in 2018—also share son Leo, 6, and daughter Ariana, 4.

And while Quentin, 63, has kept his family life rather private, he has described fatherhood as an emotional experience.

“I can’t even see his name written on a piece of paper without crying,” the Once Upon a Time... in Hollywood filmmaker told CBS This Morning in July 2021, referring to Leo, whom he praised as the “most charming human being I’ve ever met in my life.”

“Half the time, I look at him and I’m just laughing because he’s so funny,” he continued. “And the other time, I’m just bursting into tears.”

And though Quentin’s first born shares a first name with one of his frequent collaborators Leonardo DiCaprio, he and his wife didn’t choose the moniker because of the Titanic star.

“We almost didn't name him [Leo] because people would assume that I named him after Leonardo DiCaprio,” Quentin shared during a June 2021 appearance on Jimmy Kimmel Live. “There's nothing wrong with that but I didn't.”

“He's named after my wife's grandfather,” Quentin said, “but also because, just in our hearts, he was our little lion.”

And for the Pulp Fiction director—who splits his time between Los Angeles and his wife’s native Tel Avi, Israel—fatherhood has been his top priority.

After all, Quentin revealed last year that he was delaying his tenth and final film to focus on his family.

“The idea of jumping on a voyage when they’re too young to understand it is not enticing to me," the Hateful Eight filmmaker said of his kids at the 2025 Sundance Film Festival, per People. “I want to not do whatever movie I end up doing until my son is at least 6. That way he’ll know what’s going on, he’ll be there, and it will be a memory for the rest of his life.”

Book a Room With a Kitchen – Cooking Breakfast Can Cover the Higher Rate

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

For a family trip of three nights or more, compare a hotel suite, extended-stay room, or rental with a kitchen to a standard room. Add up the kitchen’s extra cost for the whole stay, any cleaning fee included, and book the one with the kitchen if cooking breakfast and a few dinners would save your family more than that.

That extra cost shows up in the nightly rate. The savings show up at every meal your family doesn’t buy at a restaurant.

Say a room with a kitchen costs your family of four $30 a night more than one without. Breakfast out costs $50 and dinner $100, against $15 and $30 for the same meals from a grocery store. Cooking breakfast saves $35 a day, which covers the $30 on its own.

Over five nights, cooking every breakfast and two dinners saves $315, or $165 after the kitchen’s extra $150. On a stay of a night or two, the kitchen often doesn’t pay off, because some costs come once per stay, such as the coffee, oil, and spices from your first grocery run. A rental’s cleaning fee is one of them. A $100 fee adds $50 a night to a two-night stay, more than the $35 a day you save by cooking breakfast, but only $20 a night to a five-night one.

Extended-stay chains put a kitchen in every room. Extended Stay America’s suites all have full kitchens, and the chain discounts longer stays. Candlewood Suites’ studios and suites come with full kitchens too. Some extended-stay chains also serve a free breakfast, so there the kitchen saves you money only on the other meals you cook. Check for a stovetop, since some kitchenettes have only a microwave.

On Airbnb or Vrbo, compare a rental’s total price for your dates with a hotel’s. Under the Federal Trade Commission’s fee rule, that total must include the cleaning fee, as a hotel’s must include any resort fee, though taxes can be added before you pay.

Shop on day one for breakfasts and two or three dinners that share ingredients, such as taco meat one night and burrito bowls the next. Plan a dinner out every day or two so nobody spends the trip at the stove.

If cooking breakfast saves your family more than the kitchen’s extra cost per night, with any cleaning fee divided across your nights, the kitchen pays for itself before you cook a single dinner.

To find a room with a kitchen for your dates, compare the best hotel booking apps.

RPV: Why Value May Lose Its Advantage

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Ronald Reagan said you can achieve anything—if you don’t care who gets the credit. Abdul El-Sayed channels that wisdom with ‘TrumpCare for all’

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The Democratic legislative agenda is taking shape, and healthcare is at the top as rising medical expenses were a major driver of midterm election messaging about affordability.

But while Democrats will hold commanding majorities in the House and Senate when the new Congress convenes in January, President Donald Trump has reminded them that he is ready to veto legislation he doesn’t like.

Senator-elect Abdul El-Sayed—a former Detroit health official who campaigned on Medicare for all—sees a way around that by appealing to Trump’s ego. On Nov. 12, he posted to social media a picture of a notional, gold-colored “TrumpCare” card, saying “Donald, this is on the table. Guarantee healthcare for every American, from cradle to grave.”

The next day, El-Sayed doubled down, assuring CNN in an interview that he wasn’t joking and insisting that he doesn’t care what the public insurance program called.

“What you call it is secondary,” he added. “What it does in your life, that’s the whole point. None of us ran to name things, except for Donald Trump. Most of us ran to actually solve problems in people’s lives.”

That got Trump’s attention. And on Saturday, he reposted on his Truth Social account El-Sayed’s initial Nov. 12 appeal. El-Sayed posted “Let’s get to work, then.”

The gambit echoes a famous line from President Ronald Reagan in February 1988, when he spoke at a meeting of the White House Conference for a Drug Free America:

“On my desk in the Oval Office, I have a little sign that says: There is no limit to what a man can do or where he can go if he doesn’t mind who gets the credit.”

While similar statements have been attributed to others going back more than 100 years, Reagan’s line is notable as it came during the modern era of American politics, which has grown even more intensely partisan since then.

In fact, during the midterm election campaign, Trump ramped up the partisan rancor to such a level that he called El-Sayed a “jihadist” who was “on the side of the terrorists.”

But the incoming Democratic senator is ready to set that aside for the sake of expanding health coverage. On Sunday, El-Sayed noted Trump “signaled openness to the opportunity,” telling NBC’s Meet the Press with Kristen Welker that while growing up playing lacrosse, he was taught to play the ball, not the man.

“If the president is serious about leaving a lasting legacy, there is nothing better than the ultimate Trump card, a card that they can have in their back pocket from the moment they are born to the moment that they pass away that guarantees them the health care that has been so elusive in this country without premiums, co-pays, and deductibles,” he said.

El-Sayed also predicted that if Trump says he wants TrumpCare for all and is open to signing legislation, then a lot of Republicans in Congress will vote for it.

When asked later if he has any plans to meet with Trump or Vice President JD Vance, El-Sayed replied “those plans are in the works.”

Minnesota Senator-Elect Peggy Flanagan, another Democrat who rode the blue tsunami, voiced similar willingness to capitalize on Trump’s desire to name things after himself.

“Call it whatever you want. I think people are ready for Medicare for all. If we have to call it TrumpCare in order to get it across the finish line, that’s fine by me,” she told CNN on Sunday.

The discussion over health coverage comes as open enrollment season is in full swing across the country, with workers getting sticker shock on how much premiums are going up for next year.

But Americans who get health insurance through their employers aren’t the only ones feeling the pain. The cost of coverage via Affordable Care Act plans is surging too after Congress didn’t renew the enhanced subsidies that were enacted during the pandemic.

If Trump makes a deal with Democrats to expand health coverage, it would be a 180 after he tried unsuccessfully to kill the Affordable Care Act during his first term.

Officially naming a new health program as TrumpCare would also mark a departure from Barack Obama’s presidency, when Republican critics of the ACA blasted it as ObamaCare.

For his part, Obama embraced that moniker but quipped in 2024 that people are calling it the Affordable Care Act again.

“I noticed by the way that since it’s become popular they don’t call it ObamaCare no more,” he said at the Democratic National Convention.

Jason Ma is the weekend editor at Fortune, where he covers markets, the economy, finance, and housing.

11-term House rep who mocked a kid’s letter about EVs has lost her seat (Upd.)

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

An 83 year old Congressional representative who sent an embarrassing letter to a well-meaning 10 year old constituent on the topic of electric cars and climate change, where she seems to brag about saddling his generation with debt through a recent climate rollback she voted for, has lost her seat.

Update: This article was originally posted on May 14, but we wanted to update it with news of the election result. After a recount, Chuck Hubbard has defeated Virginia Foxx by 330 votes, and will go on to represent NC-5 in Congress.

Christian Mango is ten years old, and was encouraged by his elementary school class to write a letter about a topic of his choice, and mail it to a decisionmaker. Christian chose his Congressional representative, 83 year old Virginia Foxx.

Foxx is an 11-term Congresswoman in North Carolina, one of the most gerrymandered states in the nation, and was expected to win a 12th term in the heavily republican district, having previously proudly touted an endorsement from an enemy of the US who tried to overthrow the government. Her opponent is Democrat Chuck Hubbard, a former journalist and native of the district.

For the topic of his letter, Christian chose electric cars – which is what makes this story relevant to us here at Electrek. Christian laid out several points about EVs, stating that “they’re better than normal cars. They’re better for the environment. They pay less. And there’s no gas.” (All true! well done Christian).

Christian suggested that electric cars could be incentivized with a $5,000 tax rebate – lower than the $7.5k EVs used to get, but better than the $0 they get federally today (compare to the ~$20k in ignored costs that gas cars still benefit from).

Christian went on to suggest action on climate change, stating “maybe there is still a chance for 6.5 million people to not die anymore and for the weather to get better just maybe glaciers stop melting.” Christian was probably referring to the number of people who die from air pollution globally each year (though several estimates suggest that number is actually higher, and Christian did conflate climate and weather… but we’ll give him a pass on this one, his heart is in the right place).

Christian was proud of his letter, as was his mother, Emily Mango, who said “he researched, cited facts, and made good arguments in his essay – an essay that had nothing to do with politics. He was proud of his essay.”

But then he got a response, and that’s when things took a turn for the worse. Christian’s mother Emily angrily shared the response on Instagram, where it gained traction.

Emily said Foxx’s screed was “disrespectful, hostile and unkind” and called it “demoralizing and reprehensible.” She said she had to speak to her son and teach him that this response was “not okay,” and that “nobody should talk to a child like that and nobody should talk to a teacher like that.”

I’d also add that it’s full of disinformation, and that it even seems to brag about the massive debt that Foxx has imposed on Christian’s generation.

Foxx includes six links, all from propaganda publications that deny the science of climate change. 3 are from from WSJ (2 of which are opinion pieces) and 1 is from Fox, both of which are owned by the same climate denier, Rupert Murdoch. 1 is from Washington Times, a tabloid founded by a religious cult (yes, really), and 1 is from another right-wing climate denying publication, National Review.

Notably, none of the six links have anything to do with electric cars.

In the text of the response, Foxx touts republicans’ “all of the above” energy policy, which is ironic given that the party whose energy policy she touts has spent most of the last year forcing broken dirty coal plants open (which they are still doing after ruled illegal) and trying to shut down clean cheap ones. Apparently “all of the above” is code for “fossil fuels and nothing else.”

Foxx’s letter does not directly address the strengths of electric cars, despite them being the subject of Christian’s letter. Notably, EVs actually can receive energy from any generation method, whether it be wind, solar, or even coal or gas – whereas gas-powered vehicles can only use one type of energy, oil. Thus, an “all of the above” strategy should probably find EVs the preferable route for transportation.

But in apparent contradiction to Foxx’s surely sincerely held belief that we should pursue an “all of the above” energy policy – which electric cars would help to achieve – she in fact has voted against EVs when given the opportunity.

Foxx claims in her response that she can’t support tax credits on electric cars, because those go to “mostly people with higher incomes who are able to purchase ‘new’ electric cars.”

But she in fact voted against, and later voted to repeal, incentives which were limited to lower-income-earners and were only available on cars up to a certain MSRP cap. Further, they were available on used vehicles, not just new ones – suggesting that Foxx doesn’t know what she voted against.

Foxx was one of 218 republicans to vote in favor of a ~$4 trillion giveaway to wealthy elites. Her vote raised electric bills and threatened US energy security and surrendered US manufacturing jobs to China. Some of the money given away to wealthy elites in the bill was clawed back from energy efficiency programs that had been going to regular Americans.

Those energy efficiency programs had been instituted by the Inflation Reduction Act, the Biden-era law which established tax credits for EVs and other efficiency rebates. The Inflation Reduction Act was actually revenue positive, which is to say it reduced the national debt (by $238B), rather than adding to it, because of provisions to increase enforcement against wealthy tax cheats.

But most of the IRA was repealed by the bill that Foxx voted for, meaning the US lost out on those deficit reductions. All in all, the bill Foxx voted for would add $4 trillion to the national debt – which, incidentally, is another point Foxx touches on in her response.

In one sentence, she seems to directly mock Christian, telling him that “YOU and your classmates will be responsible for that debt” as part of a warning about the dangers of government debt.

But I read this sentence as mockery, rather than as a sincere warning, given that Foxx voted to add $4 trillion to the national debt, which she, at 83 years old, knows she will not have to pay off. Christian, who is 10, will likely spend his whole life saddled with the debt Foxx voted for.

He will also have to pay for her inaction on climate change, which will have increasingly worse effects on the world Christian will grow up into. Foxx’s generation has already felt the heat, with the massive floods that devastated her state in 2024, from a hurricane that was made 200x more likely due to climate change. Now, imagine how much worse it could get if Foxx’s policies remained in place until 72 years from now when Christian will be her age.

Foxx then closes out the letter with disparaging remarks about teachers, claiming that they are “too interested in indoctrinating you,” and warning Christian about “propaganda.” Which I guess shows where Foxx’s attitude comes from – if she has spent her 83 years never trusting anyone who knows more than her about a topic, that would certainly lead to the ignorance reflected in her letter.

Foxx – who somehow has a doctorate in education and currently sits on the Congressional education committee – could have turned this into a nice moment to teach the child who wrote her something about democracy and responsive government, but instead turned it into a moment to teach the world what’s wrong with her.

When we first wrote this article in May, Foxx had a projected 97% chance of winning in a district which voted for her by a 19% margin in 2024.

But that was before America (and its short memory) was reminded of how incapable republicans are of leading anything.

After a pile of economic crises caused by Donald Trump’s incompetent “leadership,” and his continued participation in an 8-month-and-counting war of choice that is spiking energy prices worldwide, bringing oil profits to record highs (and attempting to distract the news from talking about the Epstein files, which he is heavily featured in and has tried to cover up), America was once again fed up. And his incompetence took his entire party down with him.

Safe republican seats were flipped around the country in the biggest “wave” election we’ve seen in recent times, with the Democrats gaining control of both the House and Senate.

And it looks like Democrat Chuck Hubbard, a Wilkes county native and former journalist, who also ran against Foxx in 2024, will now get his chance to represent the people of North Carolina’s 5th district, after winning by 330 votes in a recount.

The situation in North Carolina is particularly interesting, given that republicans had done an emergency redraw of district lines in an attempt to cheat their way to winning more seats.

Generally, district lines get redrawn after each Census, which happens on the decade mark, rather than mid-decade. But republicans saw ahead of time that they were likely to lose the House due to their unpopularity, and wanted to fudge the numbers in several states instead of going for a fair fight.

So they engaged in Gerrymandering, a process wherein district lines are redrawn to favor a particular political party. The idea is to put as many of your opponents’ votes into a few safe seats as possible, while spreading out your own votes to a wider range of seats that you win each by a small margin.

Foxx’s main argument against Christian’s suggested $5,000 EV incentive, is increasing national debt. Yet in just one tax bill, her approval increases the national debt over 10%. This is not Christian’s “responsibility”, this burden is forced on him, along with climate change.

But it turns out that republicans were even more unpopular than they thought, and as a result, their Gerrymandering backfired. Those narrow margins they sought ended up flipping more seats than would have otherwise flipped, turning North Carolina almost completely blue in what analysts have called a “Dummymander“. (NC-5 itself was not one of the redrawn districts, though – it just swung 19 whole points to the Dems)

After a nail-bitingly close election, Foxx requested a recount. The margin is extremely thin, just 0.12% of votes, but today Foxx called to concede, making it the last decided House race of the election, giving Democrats a 258-seat majority.

Foxx previously vowed to run again in 2028, hoping for a less hostile political environment. She will be 85 years old at the time of that election.

Hyatt Keeps Pepsi For 10 More Years At 700+ U.S. Hotels—Even Though Few Guests Want It

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Hyatt has signed up for another 10 years with Pepsi, covering more than 700 U.S. hotels. After Marriott finally decided to switch to Coca-Cola, Hyatt is sticking with the beverage that prompts a question nobody wants to hear: Is Pepsi okay? No, it isn’t. I rarely drink soda. When I do, I don’t want to waste it on Pepsi. The renewal announced November 19, flagged by The Bulkhead Seat, doesn’t cover every Hyatt brand or every country. But the economics of choosing Pepsi make more sense once you realize that the person negotiating the contract isn’t the one that has to drink it. Which Hyatt Brands Are Covered, And Where Can You Get Coke? The agreement covers U.S. hotels in Hyatt’s Classics and Essentials portfolios. Translating Hyatt’s brand groupings: Portfolio Brands Classics Grand Hyatt, Hyatt Regency, Destination by Hyatt, Hyatt Centric, Hyatt Vacation Club, Hyatt Essentials Hyatt Place, Hyatt House, Hyatt Studios, Hyatt Select, Caption by Hyatt, Unscripted by Hyatt UrCove also belongs to Essentials, but its Chinese hotels aren’t covered by a U.S. agreement. The Luxury, Lifestyle and Inclusive Collection portfolios aren’t part of this deal. No one wants to show up at an Andaz or Park Hyatt and get told they can’t have a Coke. Alila, Thompson, The Standard, and Hyatt Ziva and Zilara are also outside the deal’s scope. Here are some hotels that have specifically listed Coke: Hotel Coca-Cola On The Menu Park Hyatt New York Room service lists Coke and Diet Coke Andaz Maui Meeting and event menus list Coke and Diet Coke Thompson Dallas Room service lists Coke and Diet Coke Alila Marea Beach Resort Encinitas Room service lists bottled Coke Pepsi Doesn’t Have To Be Your First Choice To Win The Business When I covered Marriott’s switch in April, Marriott told hotels that more than 70% of its guests favored the Coca-Cola portfolio. Hotels choose suppliers based on the total deal: product pricing, rebates, equipment, servicing, marketing support and sometimes direct payments. We can see how this works in a public contract. Fresno, California’s 2018 Pepsi agreement for its convention center offered a $20,000 annual sponsorship payment, a 25% vending commission, $3 rebates per case or gallon sold, bulk pricing and equipment maintenance in exchange for exclusive beverage rights. A hotel has a modestly captive audience. You’ve already booked the room, your conference is downstairs, or you’re sitting by the pool. You can buy a Coke elsewhere, but that takes effort. Some people accept Pepsi. Others choose something else. The hotel loses some beverage sales and creates some disappointment. But it still thinks it can come out ahead if its supplier prices and rebates are better and they aren’t seeing guests change their hotel bookings. In other words, the worse product can be more profitable to the hotel – or at least to Hyatt corporate. Here, Hyatt brings access to guests whose hotel decision usually turns on something other than soda. Pepsi doesn’t have to persuade all those guests that it tastes better (it doesn’t). Getting its products stocked on property can shift purchases without changing anyone’s preferences. PepsiCo also brings Gatorade, Starbucks ready-to-drink coffee, Quaker, Lay’s, Doritos, Muscle Milk and poppi. “Is Pepsi Okay?” Was Never A Compliment The question assumes you’ve requested something else and asks whether you’ll accept a substitute you didn’t want. Pepsi understood the problem well enough to build its 2019 Super Bowl commercial around it, recruiting Steve Carell, Cardi B and Lil Jon to insist Pepsi was “More Than OK.” John Belushi’s Olympia Restaurant on Saturday Night Live had already reduced the experience to “No Coke! Pepsi!” You could order what you wanted. You were getting what the restaurant had decided to sell. The Pepsi Challenge, New Coke, And What A Recipe Can’t Copy I remember the Pepsi Challenge as a kid. I remember how scared Coke seemed, and the debacle of New Coke. The explanation popularized by Malcolm Gladwell in Blink is that Pepsi’s sweetness does better in a sip test than when someone drinks a whole can. Maybe some people really did prefer the first sip of Pepsi and then found the rest too much. That never seemed right to me. The first sip of Coke is much better. Fortunately, there’s no rule you have to finish a soda. Coca-Cola’s history of New Coke says the company’s lead over its chief competitor had been slipping for 15 consecutive years. It introduced the reformulated drink on April 23, 1985, after taste tests involving nearly 200,000 consumers favored the new formula. The original came back just 79 days later. The tests hadn’t captured how people would react to having their Coke taken away. The company could produce a drink that performed better in a test and still make customers furious by replacing the one they loved. That helps explain why Coke’s business is so hard to recreate even if someone could duplicate the liquid. The secret formula gets the attention, but a competitor would also need to reproduce decades of habits, memories, advertising and confidence that the drink will be what you expect. A familiar brand changes the experience of drinking it. Then there’s the distribution system: bottlers, delivery routes, coolers, fountains and relationships with stores and restaurants. Being easy to find reinforces the habit of asking for Coke. You know there’s already a strong likelihood it’ll be there when you ask. I don’t ask for my preferred drinks outside of places I know it’s available. Would you ask for Golden Elephant Three Lotus Pure Palm Juice at a diner off the New Jersey Turnpike? I don’t drink enough soda to make defending Coke a daily activity. If I’m going to have soda, Dr. Brown’s cream is my first choice, followed by their black cherry. Hyatt can negotiate all the Pepsi it wants. I’ll just have water, thanks. The agreement covers U.S. hotels in Hyatt’s Classics and Essentials portfolios. A hotel has a modestly captive audience. You’ve already booked the room, your conference is downstairs, or you’re sitting by the pool. You can buy a Coke elsewhere, but that takes effort. Some people accept Pepsi. Others choose something else. The hotel loses some beverage sales and creates some disappointment. But it still thinks it can come out ahead if its supplier prices and rebates are better and they aren’t seeing guests change their hotel bookings. In other words, the worse product can be more profitable to the hotel – or at least to Hyatt corporate. I remember the Pepsi Challenge as a kid. I remember how scared Coke seemed, and the debacle of New Coke.

The explanation popularized by Malcolm Gladwell in Blink is that Pepsi’s sweetness does better in a sip test than when someone drinks a whole can. Maybe some people really did prefer the first sip of Pepsi and then found the rest too much. That never seemed right to me. The first sip of Coke is much better. Fortunately, there’s no rule you have to finish a soda.

Coca-Cola’s history of New Coke says the company’s lead over its chief competitor had been slipping for 15 consecutive years. It introduced the reformulated drink on April 23, 1985, after taste tests involving nearly 200,000 consumers favored the new formula. The original came back just 79 days later.

The tests hadn’t captured how people would react to having their Coke taken away. The company could produce a drink that performed better in a test and still make customers furious by replacing the one they loved.

That helps explain why Coke’s business is so hard to recreate even if someone could duplicate the liquid. The secret formula gets the attention, but a competitor would also need to reproduce decades of habits, memories, advertising and confidence that the drink will be what you expect. A familiar brand changes the experience of drinking it.

Then there’s the distribution system: bottlers, delivery routes, coolers, fountains and relationships with stores and restaurants. Being easy to find reinforces the habit of asking for Coke. You know there’s already a strong likelihood it’ll be there when you ask. I don’t ask for my preferred drinks outside of places I know it’s available. Would you ask for Golden Elephant Three Lotus Pure Palm Juice at a diner off the New Jersey Turnpike?

I don’t drink enough soda to make defending Coke a daily activity. If I’m going to have soda, Dr. Brown’s cream is my first choice, followed by their black cherry. Hyatt can negotiate all the Pepsi it wants. I’ll just have water, thanks.

Legendary Jewelry Designer Paolo Bulgari Dead at 89

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Paolo Bulgari—whose Bvlgari Group created unforgettable jewelry designs for stars like Elizabeth Taylor and Zendaya—has died at age 89.

Paolo Bulgari—famed jewelry designer and chairman of the Bvlgari Group—has died, the company confirmed on social media Nov. 21. He was 89.

“It is with deep sadness that we learned of the passing of Paolo Bulgari, grandson of the company’s founder Sotirio Bulgari and Chairman of the Bvlgari Group since 1984,” the company said in a statement shared to Instagram. “Our deepest condolences go to his family, friends and all those who had the honor of sharing this journey with him.”

“Thank you, Mr. Paolo,” they added, “your legacy as a brilliant beacon of creative inspiration will continue to inspire generations to come.”

Born in Rome, Italy, in 1937, Bulgari—who shared four kids with ex-wife Alice Valli as well as two kids with wife Maite Caprio—was destined to carry on the family business from a young age.

“Paolo Bulgari began collaborating with his father in 1960,” the statement explained, “specializing in the knowledge of precious stones, of which he was one of the world’s leading experts, and refining the sensibility and aesthetic sense that are at the root of the Bvlgari style.”

And it wasn’t long until he started making his own mark on the brand.

“A true creative soul, he poured his heart into his craft and into nurturing the talents of young designers,” added the statement, “leaving an indelible mark on the world of jewelry.”

In fact, Bulgari, alongside his brothers Gianni Bulgari and Nicola Bulgari, played an integral role in shaping the brand’s distinctive style, becoming known for their use of bold shapes and standout gems.

They also expanded the business from jewelry into watches, perfumes and even hotels.

And over the decades, the brand’s creations have adorned stars from Elizabeth Taylor and Princess Diana to Zendaya and Anne Hathaway.

Just last month, Kim Kardashian said she might just “faint” in a video shared to Instagram Oct. 28 while getting a private tour of Taylor’s jewelry collection at the Bvlgari store in Italy, in which she was able to try on one of the late actress’ beloved pieces—an emerald and diamond necklace gifted to her by Richard Burton in 1962.

As Kardashian put it, “I am freaking out!”

And she of course had to thank her stylist Law Roach for making the dream come true, calling him on the phone to thank him for the “best” birthday present ever.

And while it’s true that Bulgari’s influence on fashion has stood the test of time, that’s because he believed “everything can be a source of inspiration.”

As he used to say, per Bvlgari’s statement, “There are no rules in creativity. Never.”

What Every 65-Year-Old Should Know About Social Security

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

It's below full retirement age, but still a point where key choices need to be made.

By Stefon Walters – Nov 22, 2026 at 2:30PM EST

Age 65 can feel like a gray area for Social Security because it's a few years after you can start receiving benefits, but it's still before you reach your full retirement age. For a while, 65 was the traditional retirement age and seen as the "finish line," but that isn't necessarily the case today.

But age 65 still matters because it affects Social Security benefits and has health insurance implications, so if you're nearing 65 or planning to claim at that age, here are a few things to know.

You can begin claiming Social Security at age 62, but claiming any time before your full retirement age -- which is 67 for anyone born in 1960 or later -- will reduce your monthly benefit. Your full retirement age is when you can receive your full monthly benefit, called your primary insurance amount. This is the starting point Social Security uses to determine your benefit based on when you claim.

Benefits are reduced by 5/9 of 1% monthly for each month you claim early, up to 36 months. So, if your full retirement age is 67 and you claim at 65, your benefit will be reduced by around 13.33%. If your full benefit was $2,000, you'd receive $1,733.33 if you claimed at 65.

If you claim benefits before your full retirement age, you could be subject to the Social Security retirement earnings test if you earn above a certain limit. For 2026, the limit is $24,480 if you won't reach your full retirement age in 2026 and $65,160 if you will. In 2027, these limits increase to $25,560 and $67,920, respectively.

If you earn above the limit in the year you won't reach full retirement age, your monthly benefit will be reduced by $1 for each $2 you're over. If you reach full retirement age that year and earn above the limit, your benefit is reduced by $1 per $3 you're over.

Thankfully, benefits aren't permanently lost -- just withheld. Once you reach your full retirement age, Social Security will gradually return the withheld benefits by increasing your monthly payout.

Social Security and Medicare are two different programs, but they're connected in how premiums are paid. You become eligible for Medicare at age 65, and if you're receiving Social Security at the time, your Medicare Part B premiums are automatically deducted from your monthly Social Security benefit.

This year, the Medicare Part B premium is $202.90, but it will increase to $209.40 in 2027. If you claim both Social Security and Medicare and your monthly benefit is $2,000 next year, you'd receive $1,790.60 after the premium is deducted.

This automatic deduction applies only to Part B premiums. If you're enrolled in another Medicare plan, such as Part C (Medicare Advantage) or Part D (prescriptions), you'll pay those premiums directly.

Your 401(k) Has New Contribution Limits for 2027. Here's What You Need to Know

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Workers will be able to contribute an additional $1,000 to their plans next year.

By Stefon Walters – Nov 22, 2026 at 2:30PM EST

At this point, 401(k)s have become synonymous with retirement accounts because so many Americans have one. It's by far the most used retirement account in the U.S., and it's why millions can have a financially comfortable retirement.

A 401(k) is a true win-win. It helps you save and invest for retirement proactively, and you get a tax break for doing so. There are many moving parts to a 401(k), but one key rule to keep track of is the contribution limit. Let's dive into what to expect next year.

Beginning next year, you can contribute $25,500 to a 401(k), up from this year's $24,500 limit. If you're 50 or older, you can add an $8,500 catch-up contribution (up from $8,000), bringing your limit to $34,000.

Workers aged 60 to 63 can make a special catch-up contribution of up to $11,750, meaning anyone in that four-year age range can contribute $37,250 to their 401(k)s in 2027. Once you turn 64 or older, you go back to the standard catch-up amount.

The contribution limit applies only to your personal contributions. If your job matches a certain amount of your contributions, that match doesn't count against your limit. For example, if you're younger than 50 and your job matches up to $15,000 annually, you can also contribute $15,000, even though $30,000 is above the $25,500 limit for 2027.

Not many people can afford to max out their 401(k)s year in and year out, but you should try to contribute at least the amount your employer will match. It's essentially "free" money because it's a guaranteed instant 100% return on your investment. Don't leave it on the table.

IRA contribution limits often increase, but they're staying put next year. The contribution limit for IRAs -- both traditional and Roth combined -- is $7,500. People 50 or older can add a $1,100 catch-up contribution, bringing the limit to $8,600.

A couple of changes, however, are the income limits for contributing to a Roth IRA or claiming a traditional IRA deduction. Below are the new income limits to be eligible to contribute to a Roth IRA:

Between $158,000 and $173,000 (single filers) and $250,000 and $260,000 (joint filers), there's a phase-out range where your contribution limit is gradually reduced.

Here are the new income limits to be eligible to deduct your traditional IRA contributions:

Similar to a Roth IRA, there's a phase-out range where your deduction limit gradually reduces before disappearing. They're $83,000 to $93,000, $133,000 to $153,000, and $250,000 to $260,000.

Inflation Shrinks What Your Cash Buys – Invest Money You Won’t Need for 5 Years

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

Inflation, the steady rise in prices, shrinks what each dollar you hold in cash can buy, even at the modest pace the Federal Reserve aims for. Keep your emergency fund and money you’ll spend within five years in savings, and invest the rest, since stocks have grown faster than prices over long periods.

The Federal Reserve aims for inflation of 2% a year over the long run, and even that pace doubles prices in about 35 years, so a dollar kept in cash buys half as much. In the 12 months through June 2022, prices rose 9.1%, the biggest jump in 40 years, the Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS) reported.

Prices climb when spending outruns what businesses can supply, or when costs like energy and wages rise and get passed on to shoppers. Expectations push them up too, since people who expect higher prices ask for bigger raises and buy sooner. Savers holding cash and retirees on a pension that never rises lose ground. Borrowers with fixed-rate loans gain, since they repay in dollars that buy less, and workers whose raises keep pace hold their own.

Prices rose about 92% from January 2000 to December 2025, by the government’s Consumer Price Index. By the end, $10,000 left in a checking account that paid no interest bought only what about $5,200 bought in 2000, as the BLS’s CPI Inflation Calculator shows. Interest shrinks that loss, but only a rate above inflation, a positive real interest rate, adds buying power. So sort your money by when you’ll spend it.

Keep your emergency fund and money you’ll need within five years in a high-yield savings account, where it earns interest and can’t drop with stock prices. Invest the rest in low-cost index funds, which own hundreds or thousands of stocks at once, through your 401(k), an individual retirement account (IRA), or a brokerage account.

With dividends reinvested and before fees and taxes, $10,000 invested in the S&P 500, an index of 500 large U.S. companies, grew to about $73,900 over those years, by our math from NYU professor Aswath Damodaran’s return data. That $73,900 bought what about $38,500 bought in 2000, despite a 37% loss in 2008, which is why only money you can leave alone for years belongs in stocks.

Total your emergency fund and the next five years of planned spending, keep that much in savings, and invest every dollar beyond it.

To choose where that money goes, compare the best online brokers.

The 101 money moves that matter most, from the foundations to protecting what you’ve built.

What Are The World’s Tallest & Highest Hotels? The Answer Is Tricky…

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

There’s something I really love about staying in a hotel room on a really high floor, where you’re so high up that you almost feel like you’re in an airplane. So, what are the tallest and highest hotels in the world? I want to talk about that in this post, and also discuss why the answer isn’t that straightforward.

The question of which hotel is the tallest and highest isn’t as straightforward as you might assume. And even once we do define it, there’s the question of the extent to which the answer is actually useful. First let’s cover the distinction between the tallest hotel and the highest hotel.

Generally speaking, the tallest hotel would be defined as the tallest building where a large percent of the structure is made up of the hotel. Most commonly, I see people suggest that this would include situations where at least 80% of the building is the hotel.

This is different than the highest hotel, which would be the hotel that is highest above the ground, even if it only takes up a small percentage of a mixed-use building, where there might be apartments, residences, etc.

In other words, a 50-story building that consists exclusively of a hotel would fall into the former category, while a 110-story building where the top 10 floors are a hotel would fall into the latter category.

With that out of the way, let’s talk about the properties that are remarkable in these categories…

First, let’s cover some of the world’s tallest hotels. We’ll define this as hotels where at least 80% of the building consists of the hotel facility.

Let me of course mention one downside of very tall standalone hotels — you could end up being on a very low floor. After all, if a high-rise consists exclusively (or almost exclusively) of hotel facilities, some people will end up on very low floors. The other downside, of course, is that these hotels are necessarily huge. If you prefer boutique hotels, you won’t find any on this list.

Currently, the tallest hotel in the world is Ciel Tower Dubai. The 1,042-room hotel opened in 2024, rises to a height of 1,227 feet (374 meters), and has 82 floors.

To go over the list of the top five tallest hotels in the world:

As you can see, it’s interesting (but perhaps not surprising) that all four of the world’s tallest hotels are in Dubai.

The Conrad Shanghai, meanwhile, is located in the Shimao International Plaza, and this is the perfect example of how it’s tough to define what is technically the tallest. While the building it’s in rises to such a high height, roughly 25% of that height is the spire. So technically it’s a very tall building, but the usable space isn’t as high as you’d assume.

If you ask me, the world’s highest hotels are much more interesting than the world’s tallest hotels. That’s because the world’s highest hotels typically take up the top floors of some gravity-defying skyscrapers, and therefore, they can also be boutique properties.

When defining the world’s tallest hotels, there are some general challenges:

Because of the above, there are some minor disagreements as to which hotel wins the title, but I’ll share my take (while acknowledging that I don’t have the ability to scale buildings with a measuring tape).

Currently, the highest hotel is the Rosewood Guangzhou. The 251-room hotel opened in 2019, and it’s located at the top of the CTF Finance Centre Guangzhou, which reaches a height of 1,739 feet (530 meters).

To go over the list of the top five highest hotels in the world:

As you can see, those last two properties are kind of hard to judge, and particularly J Hotel Shanghai is tricky, given that the property is split between different parts of the building.

As the inverse of the above, I should mention that the Armani Hotel Dubai is located within the Burj Khalifa, which is the world’s tallest building. However, it’s located at the very bottom of the building, so that’s hardly that useful, for these purposes, since the hotel only goes as high as 39 floors.

Lastly, in the interest of being thorough, if we’re going to define a hotel as being “highest” based on the altitude above sea level, then we’d of course end up with a completely different list. Hotel Tayka del Desierto is located near the Salar de Uyuni salt flats in Bolivia. It’s at an altitude of 14,800 feet (4,500 meters), so it’s generally considered to be the highest up hotel in the world.

I suppose depending on how we want to define “hotel,” there would probably be some simple guest houses at Everest Base Camp that go even higher, but I don’t think that’s within the spirit of what I’m trying to cover here.

If you ask me, it’s always fun to stay in a hotel room that’s really high up. There’s an important distinction between the tallest hotels and the highest hotels, though sometimes, the exact rankings are a bit blurred.

While I’ve long been interested in this, I enjoyed researching this a bit more closely, and actually having a (sort of) definitive list. Ciel Tower Dubai is the world’s tallest hotel, while the Rosewood Guangzhou is the world’s highest hotel (unless you want to consider the 120th floor of the J Hotel Shanghai separately from the rest of the property).

Have you stayed at any of the world’s tallest or highest hotels? Does it even hold any appeal to you?

A Cheaper City Weekend – Stay Near a Train Line and Do the Pass Math

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

On a city weekend, book a room near a train line rather than downtown, ride transit instead of paying to park, and plan your days around sights that are free for visitors. Buy an attractions pass only if the places you’ll see would cost more at the gate.

The easy version of a city weekend, a downtown hotel with the car in its garage and a ticket to every big attraction, is also the priciest. In the Loop, Chicago’s downtown, the Palmer House Hilton charges $57 a night just to self-park.

Good transit means you need neither a car nor a downtown room. A hotel near a train or bus stop a few miles out often costs less than one downtown, and so can a hostel’s private room.

Two other savings need a check before you count on them. A museum’s free day may be for local residents only, and an attractions pass saves money only on places you’d have paid to see anyway.

In Chicago, a CTA 3-day pass costs $15 and covers 72 hours of buses and trains, including the Blue Line ride from O’Hare that costs $5 without a pass. Buy it from a vending machine in the O’Hare station. A taxi from O’Hare to downtown averages $50, the airport says. Stay near a Blue Line stop such as Logan Square, and the same train takes you to the Loop.

The Art Institute, the Field Museum, and the Shedd Aquarium save their free days for Illinois residents. Lincoln Park Zoo and the National Museum of Mexican Art are free to everyone, and Chicago Greeter volunteers lead free hour-long walks from the Chicago Cultural Center.

Chicago CityPASS costs $144 per adult for five attractions over nine days, with upgrades such as faster entry at several of them. See just three, such as the Skydeck, the Art Institute, and the Field Museum, and general admission without the upgrades starts at $94 for all three, $50 less than the pass.

He played for Bolton Wanderers and Aston Villa - Then won Celebrity Big Brother

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Former Premier League star and Serbian international Sasa Curcic earned both fame in his home country and a substantial payout after becoming a reality TV star following his retirement from the game.

As professional footballers reach the end of their playing careers, many are left with difficult decisions over what to do with their futures. Even the luckiest of players only make it until their mid-to-late thirties in the professional game, and the question of what to do after playing contracts dry up is a very real one.

For some, coaching or scouting is an option, while for others there is a clear appeal to a post-playing career in the media. By taking these routes, former players can rely on the experience that they picked up over the decades during which they were involved in the game. Others enter into business, being able to invest as a result of their time in the game.

But for some players, what's missing is the roar of the crowd, and in the case of the former Aston Villa, Bolton Wanderers and Crystal Palace star Sasa Curcic, the former player found a whole new audience as a reality TV star once his playing days were over.

Sasa Curcic arrived in England having already impressed in his home city of Belgrade. Having first signed for OFK Beograd in 1991, he moved to Partizan Belgrade two years later, having already made his debut for the FR Yugoslavia — now Serbia & Montenegro — national team at the very start of his professional career.

In the summer of 1995, Bolton Wanderers had just been promoted to the Premier League and were looking to strengthen. Curcic was an obvious talent, so was signed by Bolton’s managerial team of Colin Todd and Roy McFarland as they sought to avoid a quick return to the second-tier.

What are your memories of Sasa Curcic? Let us know in the comments below!

Described by When Saturday Comes as "the greatest thriller at Burnden Park since the iconic Frank Worthington", Curcic was given a free role in the Bolton midfield, but he was unable to keep his team in the Premier League; they were relegated back in bottom place in the table.

But having tasted life in the Premier League with Bolton, Curcic wanted to stay there following the club's relegation, and at the start of the 1996-97 season he left for Aston Villa. It was a disastrous move. Curcic fell out with manager Brian Little, and he left the club in March 1998 for Crystal Palace with just 29 League appearances under his belt. The move from Bolton to Villa was later ranked by The Times as the 12th-worst in the first ten years of the Premier League.

But life at Selhurst Park proved to be no easier for him. He managed just 23 League appearances for Palace before leaving the club in 1999, with his spell in South London best-remembered for his protests against NATO bombing of Serbia while he was playing for Palace. After spells in Major League Soccer and in Scotland with Motherwell, he confirmed his retirement as a player in 2001.

FLW ranks the eight most famous footballers to have played in the Championship at some point in their career.

Having picked up a total of 14 appearances for his national team, Curcic was still well-known in Serbia, and he capitalised on that when he entered the Celebrity Big Brother house for their version of the show in May 2007.

As one of thirteen contestants, Curcic proved that he was still able to get people on his side, and it was confirmed a month later that he'd won the competition, scooping a prize of €50,000 and earning himself some lucrative television commercial work as a result of it.

But fame wouldn't last for him. He left the set of another reality show called Farma — "The Farm" — after just a couple of weeks the following year and faded from view again, eventually resurfacing back in South London in 2015, forming an academy with connections to Crystal Palace. He also had a spell managing, back in his home country. Appointed into the head coach's position at the Belgrade-based club Borac Lazarevac in November 2021, he lasted six months in the position before leaving.

Always a bit of a wild child — Brian Little would later tell a few stories of the midfielder's eccentricities, including "hiring a double-decker booze bus with models for his Aston Villa teammates" — Curcic himself later admitted that moving to England in the first place had been a bit of a mistake: "My dream was to play for Partizan, my team. I should have stayed. I would have my own stand, I’d be manager."

It speaks volumes for the self-confidence that got him to the top of the game in England and his subsequent fall from grace that he continued to believe in the certainty that his career would have ended up so differently had he stayed in Serbia in the first place. And Bolton Wanderers fans of a certain age certainly still remember the role he played following their original return to the Premier League.

GLP-1s Like Ozempic May Boost Brain Health, But Can They Lower Dementia Risk?

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Follow Healthline on our socials for all things health.

GLP-1 medications have transformed obesity and type 2 diabetes treatment. Now, researchers are investigating whether the effects of this blockbuster class of drugs extend beyond weight loss and affect brain health.

A comprehensive new review published on November 5 in the journal Science evaluated the current evidence on how GLP-1 medications like semaglutide and tirzepatide may affect the brain across aging, cardiometabolic disease, and Alzheimer’s disease.

The authors of the review suggested that GLP-1 medications like Ozempic and Zepbound may offer brain health benefits. However, they were also left with the question of whether the medications act directly on the brain or whether better brain health is actually a consequence of improved cardiometabolic health.

Sheena Aurora, MD, vice president, Medical Affairs, Alzheimer’s Association, who wasn’t involved in the review, told Healthline that more research is needed to better understand the effects of GLP-1 drugs on the brain.

“While research suggests GLP-1s could reduce brain inflammation, improve blood vessel health, and even protect neurons, much broader, randomized clinical trials on GLP-1s and brain health, including in people with Alzheimer’s disease and other dementias, are needed,” she said.

GLP-1 medications like semaglutide, the active ingredient in Ozempic and Wegovy, mimic the GLP-1 hormone. Tirzepatide, the active ingredient in Mounjaro and Zepbound, also targets a second hormone pathway called GIP.

These medications have been shown to produce significant weight loss and improvements in cardiometabolic health.

This is important for brain health because obesity, type 2 diabetes, and vascular dysfunction are all linked with cognitive impairment and dementia. Insulin resistance and chronic inflammation may also link metabolic and brain health.

Analyses of large outcome trials and observational cohorts have suggested that GLP-1 medications may be associated with a reduced risk of cognitive impairment and dementia. However, randomized trials in diseases like Alzheimer’s disease have yet to demonstrate clinical benefits.

Around 200 preclinical and mechanistic studies have shown benefits for learning, memory, synaptic plasticity, and neuronal resilience, though results are heterogeneous in design and direction, so a clear consensus on magnitude and mechanism remains lacking.

It is also unclear how well GLP-1 medications reach the brain. They appear to reach certain areas, such as the hypothalamus, brainstem, and circumventricular organs, more readily than regions protected by the blood-brain barrier.

The report authors noted that some studies have shown that GLP-1 receptor agonists may reduce the risk of cognitive impairment and dementia in people with type 2 diabetes. This may be due to cognitive issues being linked to factors such as blood sugar, blood pressure, vascular health, and sleep.

Mir Ali, MD, medical director of MemorialCare Surgical Weight Loss Center at Orange Coast Medical Center in Fountain Valley, CA, who wasn’t involved in the report, told Healthline, “These findings are interesting, but like many of the other benefits associated with GLP-1 medications, a direct causal relationship has not yet been proven.”

“Rather, the benefits appear to be more closely tied to the reduction in obesity and better control of diabetes,” he said.

Aurora noted that growing evidence supports the heart-brain connection.

She cited new findings from the Alzheimer’s Association’s landmark U.S. POINTER study presented at the Clinical Trials in Alzheimer’s Disease (CTAD) conference November 16–19 in Boston, MA.

The research showed that the structured lifestyle intervention program also improved heart health risk factors, such as:

“U.S. POINTER can change those risk factors within 24 months, and better heart health risk-factor control was linked with better cognition,” Aurora said. “We’re also interested to see whether adding a drug like a GLP-1 to a U.S. POINTER-style lifestyle program improves cognition in at-risk older adults more than the program alone.”

To find out, the Alzheimer’s Association is funding the Prevention of Risk for Cognitive Decline through Combined Therapy (PROTECT-Cog) global phase 3 clinical trial.

PROTECT-Cog is a first-of-its-kind trial designed to evaluate whether combining a proven multidomain lifestyle intervention with a metabolism-targeting drug, like a GLP-1, can further reduce the risk of cognitive decline, mild cognitive impairment, and dementia in at-risk older adults.

Dung Trinh, MD, internist at MemorialCare Medical Group and Chief Medical Officer of Healthy Brain Clinic in Irvine, CA, who also wasn’t involved in the review, told Healthline, “I often remind my patients that what’s good for the heart is generally good for the brain.”

“The brain depends on a healthy cardiovascular system to deliver a constant supply of oxygen, glucose, and other nutrients. Conditions such as high blood pressure, diabetes, obesity, and high cholesterol can damage blood vessels, promote chronic inflammation, and increase the risk of stroke and vascular cognitive impairment,” he said.

“Over time, these changes can also contribute to the development or progression of dementia.”

Trinh added that while GLP-1 medications can improve several cardiometabolic risk factors, these improvements do not automatically translate into proven dementia prevention.

“Regular physical activity, a heart-healthy diet, adequate sleep, and management of cardiovascular risk factors remain essential strategies for maintaining brain health as we age,” he said.

When the report authors turned their attention to more stringent tests on semaglutide in people already living with early Alzheimer’s disease, the picture began to change.

Novo Nordisk’s EVOKE and EVOKE + Phase 3 Trials examined the efficacy of semaglutide in people with early stage Alzheimer’s disease.

The studies showed that while semaglutide produced changes in several disease-related biomarkers, it didn’t slow the progression of Alzheimer’s disease.

This emphasizes that fixing metabolic issues might prevent cognitive decline in someone with obesity or diabetes. But it might not be enough to reverse the damage in someone with established Alzheimer’s disease, where profound synaptic loss and protein pathology have already occurred.

The review authors suggest that future research on obesity and diabetes should also include rigorous and specific cognitive testing.

Startup Pitch Competitions Pay Equity-Free Cash – Enter the Ones You Can Win

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

Startup pitch competitions award cash, feedback, and investor introductions without taking a share of your company. Enter contests limited to your industry, region, school, or stage of growth, where the field is small, and skip any whose prize isn’t worth the travel and weeks of preparation. Read the rules first, since a few prizes are investments that take equity.

Universities, cities, corporations, and conferences all run these contests. For a founder too early for a bank loan or an investor’s check, a well-chosen one is among the cheapest sources of money and feedback.

Your odds depend on who else can enter. A contest limited to your school, region, industry, or stage of growth draws from a smaller pool than one open to every startup. MIT’s $100K competition, for one, awards a $100,000 grand prize without taking a share of the company, and its rules admit only teams with a current MIT student.

Grow-NY, a food and agriculture contest in upstate New York, shows what to look for in the rules. Its $1 million top prize comes in stages as the winner hits milestones. The winner gives Cornell University, which runs the contest, either a right to 3% of the company or a promise of future shares. It must also locate or do business in the region for at least 12 months.

Then weigh each prize against what competing costs you: the entry fee, the travel, and the weeks you’ll spend on slides and practice instead of on customers.

Build the pitch around a clear problem, the customer who has it, and early proof that customers want what you sell, such as sales, a trial run, or a waitlist. Cut it to the time limit, look up who’s judging so you can lead with what they care about, and expect questions about your numbers.

Picture a student team that enters three regional contests in one spring. It loses the first, wins $15,000 in the second, and at the third meets an angel investor, someone who invests her own money in startups. A year later, she leads the team’s first funding round.

Win or lose, leave with the judges’ feedback and their contact information. Ask the organizer for your scores and the judges’ comments. Within two days, send each judge who asked a hard question a better answer and a short update on your progress.

Realty Income Pays Dividends Every Month. Here's What You Can Expect $10,000 Invested to Pay Monthly in 2027.

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Realty Income has paid a monthly dividend every month for more than 56 years.

By Jennifer Saibil – Nov 21, 2026 at 6:33PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Realty Income ›

If you're looking for a dividend stock, Realty Income (O +0.64%) is one of the best. It's a real estate investment trust (REIT), a business structure for real estate companies that pays out 90% of income as dividends, which is why REITs are often on best-dividend-stock lists.

Realty Income has an excellent track record, a high yield, and plenty of reasons to be confident about its future success. If you invest $10,000 right now, here's how much you can expect to get in dividends in 2027.

Realty Income has supported its top dividend by running a massive real estate business that leases properties to large essential retailers. These are chains you know and love and likely shop at, like Walmart (WMT +1.51%), Home Depot (HD +0.70%), 7-Eleven, and Dollar General (DG -2.23%).

These are stores with well-established brands and strong cash flow, making them reliable renters. They have multi-year leases, some as long as 20 years, and Realty Income's occupancy rate is a high 98.8%. It has maintained a rate between 97% and 99% since 2000.

Grocery and convenience stores make up almost 20% of the portfolio, providing a resilient revenue stream, but the company has also branched out into other areas as it expands and diversifies. The U.K. is one of its largest markets today, accounting for more than 15% of total properties, and Wynn Resorts (WYNN -2.07%) has become one of its biggest customers. Industrials make up a healthy 17% of the portfolio, and Realty Income is also getting started in data centers, which make up 1%.

The company has more than 15,500 properties in 96 industries, and it's always buying new properties to keep growing. It has a $5.5 billion revolving credit facility, and it's raised more than $2 billion so far this year from various sources. It has a large pipeline of quality properties to choose from, and expanding beyond retail offers it more opportunities.

Realty Income has paid a dividend monthly for the past 677 months, or more than 56 years. It's one of the few dividend stocks that cuts you a check every month.

It also raises its dividend quarterly rather than annually, which is unusual among great dividend stocks. It has raised the dividend for the past 117 quarters, or more than 29 years.

At the current price, the dividend yields 5.6%, an excellent yield for a reliable dividend stock.

Realty Income is a dividend play, and the stock hasn't changed much over the past 10 years. However, now has been a particularly challenging time for the company as interest and mortgage rates remain high.

Since its business model involves borrowing lots of money, higher interest rates mean it has to pay more to service its debt.

While shares are down 7% over the last six months, it doesn’t appear to be in any danger, and it’s flourished across a range of environments for more than 50 years, paying its dividend without missing a monthly payment.

As of its latest raise last week, Realty Income pays $3.288 in annualized dividends, up from $3.258. That's a small change of only 0.9%. If it continues like that for the next four quarters, by the end of 2027, it should be $3.408 in annualized dividends. The average of the two is $3.348, and divided by 12, that's an average monthly payment of $0.279.

If you invest $10,000 today at a share price of $57.66 (at the time of writing), that gets you 173 shares, and next year, you'd get about $48.27 every month.

That may not seem like much, but it's reliable and will continue to rise. It also highlights the need to save for retirement to ensure a livable monthly income.

Johnstown Mill Rats name new general manager

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

JOHNSTOWN, Pa. – The Johnstown Mill Rats announced Megan Semanchik has been named general manager.

Semanchik brings experience in sports sales, corporate partnerships, game-day operations and food and beverage management to the position, including previous roles with both the Mill Rats in the Prospect League and the Johnstown Tomahawks in the North American Hockey League.

"I'm grateful for the opportunity to take on this role and continue growing with the Mill Rats," Semanchik said.

"I've had the opportunity to see the organization from several different perspectives, and I'm excited to build on that experience.

"I'm looking forward to working with our staff, partners and community to continue growing the Mill Rats and creating a great experience at the ballpark."

Semanchik, who replaced 2025 Prospect League general manager of the year Sarah Finlay, began her professional sports career with the Tomahawks as an intern before joining the organization full time in group sales and sponsorships.

Her responsibilities extended during her time with the organization, giving her experience across multiple areas of sports business and operations.

She later joined the Mill Rats as a member of the game-day staff before moving into the role of food and beverage manager.

"We've had the opportunity to watch Megan grow within our organization, and she has consistently shown the initiative, work ethic and leadership we're looking for," said Bart Vickroy, president of Johnstown Family Entertainment, the ownership group of the Mill Rats. "She understands the business side of sports, she knows our organization and she has a strong connection to the Johnstown sports community. We're excited to have Megan take on this role and help lead the Mill Rats into our next chapter."

As general manager, Semanchik will oversee the club's day-to-day business operations, including corporate partnerships, ticket and group sales, marketing, community relations, game-day operations and the overall fan experience.

Semanchik, a Portage Area High School graduate, earned a bachelor's degree in business administration with a minor in sports management from Mount Aloysius College, completing the program in three and a half years. She was a member of the women's volleyball team and served as team manager for the men's volleyball program.
